1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
15	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
16	\label{tab:independence}
17	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
18	\toprule
19	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
20	\midrule
21	Qwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\sim}$84 & ${\sim}$0 \\
22	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\
23	Qwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\
24	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\
25	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\
26	\addlinespace
27	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\
28	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\
29	Mistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\
30	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\
31	Mistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\
32	Mistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\
33	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\
34	Mistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\
35	Mistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\
36	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\
37	\bottomrule
38	\end{tabular}
39	\end{table}
40	
41	
42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to weighted majority voting. Bias intensifies along reasoning steps (27.2\% late-step conclusion leakage vs.\ 22.0\% early, Fisher $p = 3 \times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}).
48	
49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers. This generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap ranges from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing that process-$\kappa$ uncovers hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. Interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.
59	
60	
61	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
62	\label{sec:diagnostics}
63	
64	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
68	\label{sec:remediation}
69	\label{sec:interventions}
70	
71	Twenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
72	
73	\begin{figure}[t]
74	\centering
75	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
76	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
77	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
78	\end{figure}
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	
16	\subsection{Main Results}
17	\label{sec:main-results}
18	
19	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.
20	
21	\begin{table*}[t]
22	\centering
23	\small
24	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (55.88\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$);\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$ (oracle). Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}. $^{4s}$\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.}
25	\label{tab:main_results}
26	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
27	\toprule
28	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
29	\midrule
30	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ 40\%)}} \\
31	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\
32	\midrule
33	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\sim$50--55\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\
34	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
35	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\
36	Mistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\
37	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
38	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
39	\midrule
40	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\geq$ 60\%)}} \\
41	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\
42	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
43	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
44	Qwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\
45	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\
46	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\
47	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\
48	Qwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
49	Qwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\
50	Qwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\
51	\midrule
52	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\%$)}} \\
53	QwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
54	Qwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
55	\midrule
56	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\
57	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \textbf{85.78} & $\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\times}10^{-13}${***} \\
58	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \textbf{89.71} & $\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\times}10^{-7}${***} \\
59	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \textbf{86.67} & $\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\times}10^{-15}${***} \\
60	\bottomrule
61	\end{tabular}
62	\end{table*}
63	
64	None of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation (uncorrected McNemar $p < 0.05$): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms that these trends reflect seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for 5 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation}.
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Oracle Control}
68	\label{sec:oracle}
69	
70	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: logical formulae from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\sim}$88\% lost to extraction quality.
71	
72

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART) produce significant improvements at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (SC${=}$39.33\%, gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (SC${=}$70.00\%, gap${=}{-}15.00$) achieves $+4.17$\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\leq}1.00$\,pp (n.s.). NLI escape persists even when the verifier is moderately weaker in standalone accuracy, but vanishes as the capability gap widens (${\leq}{-}18.5$\,pp). Sigmoid and linear fits yield similar adjusted $R^2$ ($0.781$ vs.\ $0.752$; $\Delta = 0.029$), so we report the monotonic trend rather than a specific inflection point. Two of four generators---from distinct model families---show statistically significant improvement (both surviving Benjamini--Hochberg correction at $m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
20	\vspace{-3mm}
21	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}
22	\label{fig:capability-gap}
23	\vspace{-6mm}
24	\end{figure}
25	
26	\begin{table}[t]
27	\centering
28	\small
29	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}
30	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
31	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
32	\toprule
33	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
34	\midrule
35	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\,pp & 0.013 \\
36	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\,pp & 0.0035 \\
37	Qwen2.5-14B$^\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.41 \\
38	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\,pp & 0.15 \\
39	\bottomrule
40	\end{tabular}}
41	\par\vspace{1mm}
42	{\footnotesize $^\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\% (same seed, different server).}
43	\end{table}

Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\\sim}$88\\% lost to extraction quality.\n\n\n",

    "oldString": "The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).",

All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nConfirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to weighted majority voting. Bias intensifies along reasoning steps (27.2\\% late-step conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% early, Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers. This generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap ranges from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing that process-$\\kappa$ uncovers hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. Interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

    "oldString": "We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.",
    "newString": "We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART) produce significant improvements at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (SC${=}$39.33\\%, gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (SC${=}$70.00\\%, gap${=}{-}15.00$) achieves $+4.17$\\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\\leq}1.00$\\,pp (n.s.). NLI escape persists even when the verifier is moderately weaker in standalone accuracy, but vanishes as the capability gap widens (${\\leq}{-}18.5$\\,pp). Sigmoid and linear fits yield similar adjusted $R^2$ ($0.781$ vs.\\ $0.752$; $\\Delta = 0.029$), so we report the monotonic trend rather than a specific inflection point. Two of four generators---from distinct model families---show statistically significant improvement (both surviving Benjamini--Hochberg correction at $m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\\,pp & 0.013 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.0035 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^\\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.41 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\\,pp & 0.15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\par\\vspace{1mm}\n{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n",

Two of four generators---from distinct families---survive BH correction ($m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (SC${=}$39.33\\%, gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (SC${=}$70.00\\%, gap${=}{-}15.00$) achieves $+4.17$\\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\\leq}1.00$\\,pp (n.s.). NLI escape persists even when the verifier is moderately weaker in standalone accuracy, but vanishes as the capability gap widens (${\\leq}{-}18.5$\\,pp). Sigmoid and linear fits yield similar adjusted $R^2$ ($0.781$ vs.\\ $0.752$; $\\Delta = 0.029$), so we report the monotonic trend rather than a specific inflection point. Two of four generators---from distinct model families---show statistically significant improvement (both surviving Benjamini--Hochberg correction at $m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\\,pp & 0.013 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.0035 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^\\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.41 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\\,pp & 0.15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\par\\vspace{1mm}\n{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n",

[Director 指令] [Patch — V14 DRC Integration, P0 优先级] DRC 数据已出，立即集成。这是 R2 novelty 的核心回应。

**Patch 1: §6 末尾新增 sub-section "Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing"**

结构（约 0.5-0.7 页）：

\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
\label{sec:drc}

**段落 1 (Motivation, 3-4 句)**:
Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions (where SC traces already converge) gain little from external verification. We propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC): a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\tau$.

**段落 2 (Method, 3-4 句)**:
For each question $Q$, we compute the SC agreement score $\kappa_Q$ as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When $\kappa_Q \geq \tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when $\kappa_Q < \tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted SC+NLI combo (w=3). The threshold $\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.

**Table (4 rows + 2 baselines)**:

\begin{table}[t]
\centering
\small
\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 93\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
\label{tab:drc}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
\midrule
SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

**段落 3 (Analysis, 3-4 句)**:
DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 93\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$pp out of $+6.67$pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\% of questions --- those where SC traces show high agreement --- are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable precisely where SC is uncertain, consistent with the Capability Gap Hypothesis: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.

**Figure**: 如果 Worker 生成了 `pareto_front.pdf`，从服务器下载到 `docs/paper/figures/` 并加入。Caption: "DRC Pareto frontier: accuracy vs. NLI invocation rate. $\tau{=}0.7$ (star) achieves the efficiency knee."。如果 figure 不可用，table 已足够，跳过 figure。

**Patch 2: Abstract 加 1 句**

在现有 NLI escape 句之后加：
"We further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 93\% of full-combo gain at 39\% of verification cost."

**Patch 3: §1 contributions list 加第 4 条**

在现有 contributions 列表末尾加：
"(iv) \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, achieving near-optimal accuracy at a fraction of the cost."

**页数**：DRC subsection ~0.6 页。如 main text 超 8 页，从 §4 SICA exhaustion 压缩（合并多个 negative direction 描述为 1 段概述 + appendix table）。

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	Across six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.
25	Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	
31	Our contributions are:
32	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
33	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
34	
35	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
36	
37	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
40	\begin{figure}[t]
41	\centering
42	\small

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Encoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (gap${=}{-}15.00$) gains $+4.17$\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\leq}1.00$\,pp (n.s.). Two of four generators---from distinct families---survive BH correction ($m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
20	\vspace{-3mm}
21	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}
22	\label{fig:capability-gap}
23	\vspace{-6mm}
24	\end{figure}
25

30	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
31	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}

Two of four generators---from distinct families---survive BH correction ($m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\\,pp & 0.013 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.0035 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^\\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.41 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\\,pp & 0.15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\par\\vspace{1mm}\n{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n",

_source line 16540 · 2026-05-21T05:08:22.168Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019YhBJnZxhSzYnrPveBDXGn`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$).
9	
```

  "new_string": "{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions---where SC traces already converge---gain little from external verification. We propose \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\\tau$.\n\nFor each question $q$, we compute the SC agreement score as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When agreement $\\geq \\tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when agreement $< \\tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted combo ($w{=}3$). The threshold $\\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\\%---where SC traces show high agreement---are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain, consistent with Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.\n"
}

The remaining 61.2\\%---where SC traces show high agreement---are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain, consistent with Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.\n",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (gap${=}{-}15.00$) gains $+4.17$\\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\\leq}1.00$\\,pp (n.s.). Two of four generators---from distinct families---survive BH correction ($m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\\,pp & 0.013 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.0035 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^\\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.41 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\\,pp & 0.15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\par\\vspace{1mm}\n{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n",

    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$).\n",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}",
    "newString": "\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 35,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 35,
        "newLines": 8,
        "lines": [
          " \\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
          " ",
          " \\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
          "+",
          "+\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).",
          " \\end{enumerate}",
          " ",
          " \\begin{figure}[t]"
        ]
      }
    ],

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost
