    "oldString": "\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
    "newString": "\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields significant gains on two of four generators ($+3.50$\\,pp, $+4.17$\\,pp; $w{=}3$), escaping the ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

Cross-architecture NLI yields significant gains on two of four generators ($+3.50$\\,pp, $+4.17$\\,pp; $w{=}3$), escaping the ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

55	
56	\begin{proposition}[Self-Extraction Ceiling]\label{prop:ceiling}
57	For generator $M$ with $\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\Delta_\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{self} \lesssim \Delta_\text{oracle} / \text{BR}(M)$.
58	\end{proposition}
59	
60	\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition]\label{cor:capability_gap}
61	Let $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.
62	\end{corollary}
63	
64	\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\ref{prop:ceiling}, the SC ceiling is bounded by $\text{BR}$. Adding $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases effective independent signals: $K_\text{eff}^\text{combo} = K_\text{eff}^\text{SC} + w_V \cdot (1 - \rho_{GV})/(1 - \rho_{GG})$, reducing the effective bias ratio. However, when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$, incorrect verifier votes counteract independence gains. The net effect follows a sigmoid in the capability gap. Empirically, our cross-architecture evaluation yields Spearman $\rho = 0.970$ ($p < 0.001$) with sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$. $\square$
```

However, when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$, incorrect verifier votes counteract independence gains. The net effect follows a sigmoid in the capability gap. Empirically, our cross-architecture evaluation yields Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$ ($p < 0.001$) with sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$. $\\square$",
    "newString": "\\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling}, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. The net effect follows a sigmoid in the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$). $\\square$",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Self-Extraction Ceiling]\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self} \\lesssim \\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition]\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{corollary}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling}, the SC ceiling is bounded by $\\text{BR}$. Adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases effective independent signals: $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo} = K_\\text{eff}^\\text{SC} + w_V \\cdot (1 - \\rho_{GV})/(1 - \\rho_{GG})$, reducing the effective bias ratio. However, when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$, incorrect verifier votes counteract independence gains. The net effect follows a sigmoid in the capability gap. Empirically, our cross-architecture evaluation yields Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$ ($p < 0.001$) with sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$. $\\square$\n",

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}
11	Our findings illuminate why the paradigm shift from test-time aggregation to trained reasoning internalization (e.g., o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode achieves $86\%$ on FOLIO, already exceeding the theoretical self-extraction ceiling of Mistral-7B (${\sim}62\%$; Proposition~\ref{prop:ceiling} with $\text{BR} = 4.97$). Rather than extracting and aggregating external symbolic structure---which inherits the generator's biases---these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the independence bottleneck entirely. Corollary~\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this boundary: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability, a condition that trained reasoning renders unnecessary.
12	
13	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
14	Two diagnostics predict failure before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with an external verifier, gain magnitude tracks the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. Z3 semantic deduplication's positive signal ($p = 0.013$; \S\ref{sec:diagnostics}) confirms the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is extraction quality, as the same mechanism succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
15	
```

Rather than extracting and aggregating external symbolic structure---which inherits the generator's biases---these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the independence bottleneck entirely. Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this boundary: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability, a condition that trained reasoning renders unnecessary.",
    "newString": "These findings illuminate why trained reasoning internalization (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves $86\\%$ on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\\sim}62\\%$; Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling}). Rather than extracting external symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck. Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}\nOur findings illuminate why the paradigm shift from test-time aggregation to trained reasoning internalization (e.g., o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode achieves $86\\%$ on FOLIO, already exceeding the theoretical self-extraction ceiling of Mistral-7B (${\\sim}62\\%$; Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling} with $\\text{BR} = 4.97$). Rather than extracting and aggregating external symbolic structure---which inherits the generator's biases---these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the independence bottleneck entirely. Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this boundary: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability, a condition that trained reasoning renders unnecessary.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with an external verifier, gain magnitude tracks the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. Z3 semantic deduplication's positive signal ($p = 0.013$; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}) confirms the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is extraction quality, as the same mechanism succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nConfirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting; bias intensifies along reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\\,pp n.s.\\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (gap${=}{-}15.00$) gains $+4.17$\\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\\leq}1.00$\\,pp (n.s.). Two of four generators---from distinct families---survive BH correction ($m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) across four models from three families.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$3.50\\,pp & 0.013 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.0035 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^\\dagger$ & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.41 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\\,pp & 0.15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\par\\vspace{1mm}\n{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions---where SC traces already converge---gain little from external verification. We propose \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\\tau$.\n\nFor each question $q$, we compute the SC agreement score as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When agreement $\\geq \\tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when agreement $< \\tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted combo ($w{=}3$). The threshold $\\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\\%---where SC traces show high agreement---are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain, consistent with Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.\n\n",

The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms that these trends reflect seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\\pm 2$\\,pp) confirm 5 conditions equivalent, 5 genuinely different (4 negative), 6 underpowered (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Looking at page 8: The main text gets through the DRC subsection, Discussion §7, and the bottom shows "Practitioner implications" paragraph starting. On page 9, I can see "8 Conclusion" starts at the top, then "Limitations" begins, followed by References.

So the Conclusion section (about 5-6 lines) is on page 9. I need to push it back to fit on page 8.

The overflow is small - just the Conclusion paragraph (~5-6 column lines) plus maybe a few lines of Discussion. Let me estimate: about 10-15 column lines overflow.


I need to be more aggressive with compression. The biggest opportunities are condensing the Conclusion from 3 sentences down to 2, tightening the DRC motivation and method paragraphs by merging them, compressing the practitioner implications further, and reducing LaTeX spacing around equations and propositions.

Looking at the page breaks more carefully, the overflow is coming from about 1-2 lines of the Discussion section plus roughly 6 lines from the Conclusion—that's around 8 column lines total, or about 0.15 of a page. The most effective fix would be to compress the DRC subsection itself since it's new content. I can merge the motivation and method paragraphs into a single, tighter paragraph instead of keeping them separate. At $\tau=0.7$, DRC recovers nearly all the accuracy gains from the full ensemble while only invoking the NLI verifier on about 39% of questions—the ones where self-consistency traces disagree. The majority of questions with high agreement are resolved correctly by self-consistency alone, which aligns with the intuition that verification helps most when the generator's internal consensus is weak. Oracle controls confirm the mechanism works, but the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself—any self-verification pipeline risks inheriting the same biases. I've identified three diagnostics that predict when trace-aggregation fails, and a cross-architecture experiment shows we can escape the mirage on some generators, though questions remain about temperature sensitivity, scaling, and closing the gap between NLI and oracle performance.
```

44	
45	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
46	\label{sec:drc}
47	
48	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions---where SC traces already converge---gain little from external verification. We propose \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\tau$.
49	
50	For each question $q$, we compute the SC agreement score as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When agreement $\geq \tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when agreement $< \tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted combo ($w{=}3$). The threshold $\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
56	\label{tab:drc}
57	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
58	\toprule
59	Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
60	\midrule
61	SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
62	DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
63	\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
64	DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
65	Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
66	\bottomrule
67	\end{tabular}
68	\end{table}
69	
70	DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$\,pp out of $+6.67$\,pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\%---where SC traces show high agreement---are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain, consistent with Corollary~\ref{cor:capability_gap}: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.
71	
72
